## Confirmed Trial Scope and Oracle Protocol

- The user authorizes considering all business flows exposed by the user-confirmed Group/GHR Test5 deployment that remain inside the stated boundary; plan the full in-boundary coverage by default rather than asking the user to narrow it. This is authorization to plan, not a frozen scenario denominator or approval of unresolved business expected values.
- For each mutating scenario, use an explicitly synthetic, uniquely identifiable entity and verify its status from the corresponding business list after every required terminal transition. Capture the unique code, list filter, returned status, and independent detail/history observation as the business Oracle; do not inspect or reuse existing HR work items as test data.
- Use the currently logged-in account for the live session, but do not infer that it can perform every requester and approver action. Verify actor/role coverage read-only before execution; any role gap remains blocked rather than being worked around with a different account or an unapproved write.
- Create, submit, handoff, approve, and close are inside the stated authorization boundary. Before cleanup, send an advance notice naming only Trial-generated synthetic entities, the cleanup action, expected effect, and timing; perform no cleanup before that notice. Preserve and report generated records until the notice is sent. Any cleanup outside those Trial-generated records, or any Beisen/OEHR side effect, requires separate confirmation.
- The review deliverable should present the complete candidate scenario set, each requirement-backed expected result, each list/detail Oracle, unresolved branches and external side effects, then request approval of the exact denominator and expected values before creating the Trial.

## Evidence Rules

- A file extension or path is not evidence. Image decoding and readable trace events are checked before strong results are accepted.
- A valid trace proves a readable artifact, not that the required business action happened. Business conformance requires a source-backed contract and bound actual values.
- Existing reports remain readable. Legacy results without these checks do not support new trust promotion.
- Chromium fixture tests validate the pipeline only; they are not evidence of production-system capability.
- The active checkout and real system data are not modified by this isolated development work. No npm release is authorized.

## Real Trial Resume Checklist

Before any live write, resume the Trial only after these remaining gates are satisfied:

- Freeze the exact requirement snapshot/revision and confirm the runtime System Brain is bound to the user-confirmed Group/GHR Test5 environment.
- Use the user's single currently logged-in Test5 account as the sole execution identity, as requested. Before execution, record the account's actual role and per-action grants from user-provided details and read-only target evidence; do not assume that login, menu visibility, or a single role grants every requester/approver action. Credentials and storage-state contents must stay in the configured auth flow and must not be pasted into chat or reports.
- A reviewed, approved `BusinessScenario` with a strong requirement-backed Oracle, explicit actor handoffs, preconditions, terminal state, and a read-only way to independently verify the business result.
- The user has authorized in-boundary create, submit, handoff, approve, and close operations; announce cleanup before performing it, and get separate confirmation for actions outside this boundary or any Beisen/OEHR writes.
- The fixed scenario IDs and whether to begin the planned three rounds. Blocked and unexecuted scenarios stay in the denominator.

Until these inputs are verified, do not create a Trial from stale auth labels, infer/approve an Oracle from historical screenshots, or attempt a business write. The second real system remains an external dependency; the synthetic order fixture does not satisfy migration acceptance.

真实 Trial 恢复前需确认：准确业务 workspace、目标系统与需求 revision；按用户要求仅使用当前已登录的 Test5 账号，并取得其实际角色及逐项操作权限说明，再以只读目标证据核实；经审核批准且带强 Oracle 的 BusinessScenario、角色交接、前置/终态，以及独立只读后置状态检查；逐项确认创建、提交、转交、审批、关闭、测试数据及清理授权；冻结场景 ID 和三轮计划。不得把登录成功或菜单可见推断为操作授权。凭据和 storage state 不得贴入聊天或报告。缺少任何一项时不创建 Trial、不进行业务写入；blocked 和未执行场景保留在固定分母中。

Trial review now revalidates linked evidence files against their stored hashes and checks a completion-time semantic snapshot covering contracts, reporter results, assurance, execution context and conformance. Changed, missing, newly referenced unpinned artifacts, or modified/missing semantic snapshots are inconclusive for metrics. The stored hash is an integrity fingerprint within the repository; independent tamper protection remains open. Multiple TestIntents in one scenario now aggregate conjunctively: each intent needs exactly one eligible, uniquely mapped case, and every case must have a qualifying result. Missing cases remain not-run; ambiguous or overlapping mappings remain inconclusive. This is metric aggregation only; it does not complete path planning or entity lifecycle validation.

## 2026-10-20 Review-time evidence revalidation

- Trial review reopens linked evidence artifacts from the workspace and checks their current hashes and readability before calculating business conformance metrics.
- An artifact changed since execution, a missing file, or a reference added without a stored validation hash makes that evidence inconclusive; stored execution evidence is not rewritten.
- Execution completion stores a canonical semantic snapshot hash with the evidence. Trial review recomputes it with the stored conformance record; legacy evidence without the hash or any altered contract/result is ineligible for measured conformance.
- Focused validation: `src/evaluation/evaluationTrials.test.ts`, `src/evaluation/evidenceRevalidation.test.ts`, `src/mcp/evaluationIntegrity.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/requirementSuiteRun.test.ts`, and `src/knowledge/service.test.ts` passed (88 tests). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, `npm run docs:build`, `npm run verify:package-contents`, and `npm run verify:package-install` passed.
- `npm run verify:l3-eval` passed its sanitized synthetic corpus (6/7 samples; historical Bug replay and real-system regression remain not measured); the release gate remains blocked on those external measurements.
- Runtime build identity injection is available on `RequirementSuiteRunService` for deterministic validation; production defaults to the process-pinned identity.

## 2026-10-20 Scheduled Trial binding

- `RequirementSuiteRunService.claimScheduled` now checks Trial status, source, system, requirement set, cases, and runtime build before taking a schedule lease. This protects both direct Facade control and the scheduled Runner, which claims through the same service.
- Facade claim-by-ID also rejects a mismatched `evaluationTrialId`. A blocked or invalid Trial can still be cancelled or have its lease released for recovery; neither action dispatches a business operation.
- Focused validation: `src/evaluation/evaluationTrials.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/requirementSuiteRun.test.ts`, `src/mcp/knowledgeFlow.test.ts`, and `src/runner/runner.test.ts` passed (90 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` passed.

## 2026-10-20 Multi-intent scenario aggregation

- Scenario metrics now aggregate every distinct `testIntentId` in the frozen scenario as a required constituent. Each intent must resolve to exactly one eligible case; a case mapped to multiple scenarios or multiple candidates for an intent is inconclusive.
- A scenario is measured as passed only when every required case has qualifying real-system evidence and conformant results. Missing constituent cases stay not-run; failed or blocked constituents cannot be hidden by a passing sibling case.
- Focused validation: `src/evaluation/evaluationTrials.test.ts`, `src/evaluation/evidenceRevalidation.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/service.test.ts`, and `src/mcp/knowledgeFlow.test.ts` passed (99 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` passed. This is fixture-based verification, not a real Trial. Accepted packages remain **0/6**.

## 2026-10-20 Explicit state path solving

- State compilation now resolves a path only from one confirmed state model when the intent identifies a unique starting state (declared precondition, otherwise the model's unique initial state) and a unique target state in expected results. It excludes forbidden edges, preserves each transition's source refs, trigger, preconditions and actor in generated steps, and compiles only a unique route.
- Missing endpoints do not produce a solved path. Multiple viable routes return ambiguity with all candidate transition refs; the compiler does not choose a route by relevance score or fall back to generic clause steps when explicit states lack a confirmed route.
- Focused validation: `src/knowledge/caseCompiler.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/service.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/attachmentPipeline.test.ts`, `src/brain/testdata.test.ts`, and `src/knowledge/testDataProvider.test.ts` passed (86 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` passed. Fixture-based only; broader process-model and real-system acceptance remain open. Accepted packages remain **0/6**.

- English and Chinese Provider guides now define the unique-lookup contract. Provider-backed fixture validation: `src/brain/testdata.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/testDataProvider.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/testDataPlanner.test.ts`, and `src/knowledge/dataLeaseResolver.test.ts` passed (40 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` passed. ## 2026-10-20 Intervention capture and autonomy metric

- User interventions can be attributed to frozen business scenarios. After completing a Trial, an operator can attest the exact frozen scenario ID set and provide review evidence through `bc_prepare action=complete-intervention-capture`.
- Autonomy remains not-measured without that attestation, if the frozen scope differs, if an intervention is added afterward, or if a user clarification lacks scenario attribution. The capture snapshots all intervention record IDs; a later entry invalidates measurement. Once measured, the numerator is only qualifying real-system conformant scenarios without an attributed user intervention; blocked and unexecuted scenarios stay in the fixed denominator.
- Clarification, user authentication/login, and manual recovery are distinct intervention categories. These events are non-invalidating, scenario-attributable interventions; manual store/source/code changes remain invalidating.
- Auth checkpoints can carry an explicit active Trial binding; completion records a `user-authentication` event with the checkpoint reference. Because legacy auth checkpoints do not identify a frozen business scenario, the event is deliberately unscoped and keeps autonomy not-measured until scenario-level attribution is available. No Trial is inferred from system ID or timing alone.
- Focused validation after explicit auth checkpoint binding: `src/mcp/knowledgeFlow.test.ts`, `src/domain/service.test.ts`, and `src/domain/repository.test.ts` passed (68 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check` passed. Synthetic code-level check only; no real Trial was run, A remains partial, accepted packages remain **0/6**.
- Focused validation: `src/evaluation/evaluationTrials.test.ts`, `src/evaluation/evaluationIntegrity.test.ts`, `src/mcp/knowledgeFlow.test.ts`, and `src/mcp/tools.test.ts` passed (85 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit`, build, docs build, package contents, and packed install smoke passed. The attestation is operator-reported completeness, not automatic capture. No real Trial was run; A remains partial and accepted packages remain **0/6**.

## 2026-10-20 Projection manifest chain validation

- Trial validation now rejects a missing, cross-Trial, branched, incomplete, cyclic, or non-current projection manifest chain; the live projection must still match the Trial-owned chain tip. The root must reference the Trial's source and source snapshot, and the source snapshot's locator hash is checked so a projection checkpoint cannot legitimize changed source content.
- Controlled checkpoints validate the existing chain before extending it, preventing a manifest from another Trial or an altered chain from being accepted as a controlled projection update. Execution binding checks the same live projection so direct, Facade, and scheduled claims fail closed on uncheckpointed drift.
- The dedicated Playwright fixture is excluded from Vitest discovery and remains runnable through `npm run verify:order-fixture`; the focused filter confirmed Vitest does not collect that spec.

- The isolated worktree initially lacked local dependencies and a configured browser executable. After `npm ci` and setting `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE` to the installed Chrome, the previously environment-failing CLI, artifact, and local agent-chain tests passed. Full `npm test` then passed all **133 test files / 997 tests**; the separate order fixture passed all 4 Chromium tests. `npm ci` reported 18 dependency advisories (1 low, 7 moderate, 9 high, 1 critical); these remain for release review.
- No real Trial was run. A remains partial and accepted packages remain **0/6**; synthetic and fixture checks do not count toward real business completion or autonomy.

## 2026-09-14 Document Suite Report

- Document suites persist `reportPath` on `CaseSuite` and expose it from `bc_run` and the active `bc_status` summary.
- Reports are written to `.brain-creator/artifacts/<system>/document-<source>/<suite>/report/suite-report.html` and include selected cases, pending/not-executed reasons, progress, BugReports, Gaps, related run IDs, and client-side search.
- The report is regenerated after suite creation, pending-action recovery, host-agent continuation, case completion, cancellation, and terminal suite transitions.
- A report is a Suite-level artifact; it is included in the Suite manifest without being mislabeled as per-case evidence.

## 2026-09-14 Unified Task Projection

- `bc_status` now exposes `executionTasks` for system- and knowledge-project-scoped views.
- The projection merges active requirement suites, document suites, pending agent tasks, test-data tasks and Brain tasks without duplicating child tasks already linked to a parent run.
- The active task includes the current case, step, page URL, role, entity reference, wait reason, stall warning, related task IDs and a deterministic next action.
- Ledger-only waiting is surfaced as `status=waiting` with the recovery action, while the legacy `summary.activeRun.executionRecovery` remains unchanged for compatibility.
- Knowledge-project views retain document suites through their system binding; linked AgentTask and TestDataTask records are checked against the parent system and project when those fields are present.
- A blocked parent takes precedence over stale pending children, authentication checkpoints have a distinct next action, and `relatedTaskIds` contains task IDs only; child-to-parent context is exposed separately.
- This projection is read-only; the write-action recovery contract is documented and verified in the following slice, while real-system continuity remains unmeasured.

## 2026-09-14 Write Action Recovery

- Run Ledger now accepts `action-planned`, `action-sent`, `action-confirmed`, `action-reconciliation-required`, and `action-reconciled` events with a stable `actionKey`.
- `sent` and `reconciliation-required` are exposed as `pendingAction` and force `reconcile-action`; a later Suite continuation cannot dispatch a new business action until the postcondition is reconciled.
- `bc_prepare action=record-execution-action` records the lifecycle for requirement or document suites. `bc_prepare action=reconcile-execution-action confirm=true` requires a postcondition and non-empty evidence references, then clears the pending action without replaying the side effect.
- Semantic, entity, postcondition, and evidence fields pass through the existing secret redaction path.
- This closes the in-process guard and audit contract. An optional `ExecutionPostconditionVerifier` can now query a target-specific postcondition during reconciliation; `autoVerify=true` records the returned evidence only when the verifier says `confirmed`. Without a configured verifier, or when verification is unavailable/not confirmed, the action remains `reconciliation-required`. A real-system adapter and browser/process interruption end-to-end test are still outstanding.

## 2026-10-20 Testdata Write Interruption Guard

- Provider tasks record a `prepared`/`sent`/reconciled phase. A task with an uncertain `sent` phase cannot be dispatched again until the host performs a read-only lookup and reconciles whether the prior operation took effect; a confirmed no-effect outcome re-arms only that same task for one controlled retry.
- The MCP Facade exposes explicit mark-sent and no-effect reconciliation actions. Requirement-suite preparation returns a distinct reconciliation-required status instead of handing the host another write action. Successful completion records the reconciled-applied phase; existing task identity and idempotency context remain stable.
- The protocol is a product-side duplicate-write guard, not proof of an external effect: `sourceRefs` are references supplied by the host and are not independently queried or artifact-validated by this Testdata service. Target-backed evidence validation and real-system lifecycle runs remain open.

## 2026-10-20 Run-Bound Cross-Case Data Leases

- Test-data tasks and leases now retain their owning `requirementSuiteRunId`; task idempotency is isolated by SuiteRun. When a consumer inherits a producer's active entity lease, Brain Creator records a separate read-only reuse lease linked with `reusedFromLeaseId` and the declared entity reference.
- Current-run preparation and immutable ExecutionPlans now resolve only leases owned by that same SuiteRun. A prior-run or unbound lease cannot satisfy a new run; its data must be looked up or prepared again, avoiding execution against a lease that another run may clean up.
- Cleanup waits for the selected consumer cases in the active SuiteRun to release their specific consumption leases. A prior run's terminal evidence cannot release a new run's lease; terminal evidence must match the current run and occur after the lease was created. Producer cleanup is resumed from the last consumer, after all selected consumers finish.
- Regression coverage uses two consumers and a producer lease, verifies producer cleanup is deferred while consumers are pending, rejects stale evidence from another SuiteRun, and releases the producer only after both current-run consumer leases complete. `src/knowledge/testDataProvider.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/dataLeaseResolver.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/requirementSuiteRun.test.ts`, and `src/mcp/knowledgeFlow.test.ts` passed (74 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` passed.
- Evidence and immutable ExecutionPlans are bound to their owning `requirementSuiteRunId` before the agent chain starts, so run-scoped lease resolution and terminal evidence attribution cannot fall back to prior runs. Focused validation passed (5 files / 88 tests); full `npm test` passed (136 files / 1039 tests), `npx tsc --noEmit`, build, docs build, package-contents verification, and packed-install/recovery/report smoke passed. The 4-test Chromium order fixture, Brain Creator order-fixture smoke, and host-agent document-suite smoke also passed; `git diff --check` passed.
- This proves fixture/service-level lifecycle control, not full browser Suite-flow or live target behavior. C remains partial; accepted packages remain **0/6**, and real Trial/business/autonomy metrics remain not measured.

Existing unavailable-verifier regression coverage in `src/mcp/executionActionFacade.test.ts` verifies that the pending action is not cleared; it does not prove target-system recovery.

## 2026-10-20 Operation-Aware Test-Data Recovery

- An uncertain cleanup now queries the exact leased entity reference. A structured `present` observation must prove exactly one match whose reference equals that lease before cleanup can be re-armed. A structured `absent` observation proves cleanup already took effect: Brain Creator releases the lease, marks the task `reconciled-applied`, and automatically continues the same Requirement Suite without dispatching a second cleanup.
- Create/lookup writes retain the inverse gate: only exact-query absence supports a retry. Host-supplied evidence refs alone cannot satisfy either branch; artifacts are re-opened, hash-checked, and bound to the task's system/query/result/provider/time. Unavailable or ambiguous outcomes stay blocked.
- Added a service regression for an already-applied cleanup with one cleanup task and a released lease, plus a Facade regression proving the Suite advances to the next queued cleanup after reconciliation. The failure/retry regression now explicitly verifies that a still-present entity authorizes a cleanup retry.
- `src/knowledge/testDataProvider.test.ts`, `src/mcp/knowledgeFlow.test.ts`, `src/mcp/tools.test.ts`, and `src/execution/staticSuiteReport.test.ts` passed (68 tests); full `npm test` passed (136 files / 1041 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit`, build, docs build, package-contents verification, packed-install/recovery/report smoke, all 4 Chromium order-fixture tests, Brain Creator order-fixture smoke, host-agent document-suite smoke, and `git diff --check` passed. No real target operation was performed; C remains partial, accepted packages remain **0/6**, and real metrics remain not measured.

## 2026-10-20 Immutable Recovery Oracle

- Reconciliation now requires an unresolved Run Ledger action and rejects changes to its system/case binding, action semantic, postcondition, or entity reference. Automatic verification inherits the stored entity reference rather than relying on a caller to repeat it. A write cannot be marked `sent` without a frozen postcondition; planned semantics, entity reference, and postcondition cannot be changed as the action advances.
- Added regressions proving a recovery request cannot replace the original submitted-state postcondition with an approved-state postcondition, entity reference is inherited into the structured observation contract, and a sent action without any frozen postcondition is rejected.
- `src/knowledge/runLedger.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/executionRecovery.test.ts`, `src/knowledge/executionTaskProjection.test.ts`, and `src/mcp/executionActionFacade.test.ts` passed (36 tests); full `npm test` passed (136 files / 1042 tests) with the installed Chrome executable configured; `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, `npm run docs:build`, and `git diff --check` passed. Real target verifier wiring and real-system continuation remain open; D remains partial, accepted packages remain **0/6**, and real business/autonomy metrics remain not measured.

## 2026-10-20 Manual Recovery Artifact Gate

Validated artifacts are persisted on the reconciled Run Ledger entry and linked from Trial intervention records. Missing artifacts, mismatched observations or replaced bytes leave the action unresolved; evidence-reference strings alone cannot resume the suite.

## 2026-10-20 Explicit State-Path Selection

- A code-path audit found that ambiguous state paths created a `state-action` ExplorationTask, but resolving the task accepted only evidence references. The compiler would encounter the same ambiguity again because there was no way to record which reviewed route was selected.

References must belong to the task's source scope, the compiler verifies they exist in confirmed state models and form the complete path between the declared precondition and target, and the chosen references are persisted on both the task and TestIntent and included in the compilation cache key. Incomplete, foreign, or invalid selections remain ambiguous; no path is chosen by scoring or array order. The selected refs are shown in the confirmation preview.

Initial test setup incorrectly called a system-profile factory absent from `KnowledgeService`, then expected a System Brain path verdict despite providing no page evidence; the fixture and assertion were corrected without weakening product gates. `src/knowledge/service.test.ts` and `src/knowledge/caseCompiler.test.ts` passed (53 tests); the final full `npm test` passed (136 files / 1,051 tests), and `npx tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check` passed.

The path regression asserts all side effects remain present; the updated full `npm test` again passed (136 files / 1,051 tests), followed by `npm run build`, fresh `npm run verify:package-install`, and `git diff --check`.

## 2026-10-21 Runner Lease Renewal Ordering

- Runner cleanup previously waited at most 50 ms for an in-flight lease heartbeat before releasing a waiting run. A delayed asynchronous renewal could therefore race with release or result reporting. Runner now awaits the actual renewal promise, and its controller contract accepts synchronous or asynchronous renewal implementations.
- Added a delayed-heartbeat regression proving release cannot happen before renewal completes. Runner, CLI, and requirement-suite service tests passed (3 files / 53 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. This improves local schedule reliability only; Runner production long-cycle validation and real business acceptance remain outstanding. Accepted packages remain **0/6**.

- After the Runner change, package contents verification passed (172 entries; 1,534,156 bytes), and the fresh `2.1.1` tarball install/startup/recovery/report smoke passed. npm publish was not run.

## 2026-10-21 Atomic Trial Recovery Recording

- Manual execution reconciliation previously persisted the terminal Run Ledger event before recording its required Trial intervention. If intervention persistence failed, retrying the reconciliation was rejected as a terminal action. Trial-bound manual reconciliation now records both inside one repository transaction, and nested sharded transactions defer disk writes to the outer commit so rollback leaves neither partial record behind.
- Added failure-injection coverage proving reconciliation can be retried after intervention recording fails, plus a sharded-store regression proving nested writes roll back with the outer transaction. `src/domain/shardedRepository.test.ts` and `src/mcp/executionActionFacade.test.ts` passed (2 files / 21 tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check` passed. Follow-up full test passed all 138 files / 1,067 tests (`--maxWorkers=1` with installed Chrome); build, package contents (172 entries; 1,534,518 bytes), and fresh `2.1.1` tarball install/startup/recovery/report smoke passed. This remains local recovery-integrity evidence, not real-system acceptance; accepted packages remain **0/6**. npm publish was not run.
- Replayed the actual sharded-store Runner entrypoint 20 times in a newly isolated temporary workspace after the lease and transaction changes: every iteration completed, the persisted iteration count reached 20/20, and active leases were 0 at each boundary. This is synthetic scheduler/control-plane recovery evidence only; it does not count as production Runner reliability or a real Trial.

## 2026-10-21 Source Build Identity Fingerprint

- Source-mode runtime identity previously used only `source:<commit>:dirty`, so distinct uncommitted working trees on the same commit were indistinguishable. It now fingerprints the tracked binary diff and the content/path of non-ignored untracked files, while retaining the commit and dirty flag. The process-local identity cache prevents repeating Git fingerprint work after startup; identity remains pinned for that process lifetime.
- An isolated Git-repository regression proves distinct tracked edits and distinct untracked file contents produce different build IDs. Runtime-identity tests passed (2 files / 5 tests), `npx tsc --noEmit` passed, and full `npm test -- --maxWorkers=1` passed (138 files / 1,072 tests). Build and package-contents verification (172 entries; 1,538,414 bytes) plus fresh `2.1.1` package-install/recovery/report smoke passed; `git diff --check` passed. This verifies source identity distinction and package installation only; real Trial execution remains outstanding, accepted packages remain **0/6**, and npm publish was not run.

## 2026-10-21 Semantic Input Freeze Hardening

- B-package audit found Trial source validation compared the source locator and the stored external `contentHash` field, but did not recompute a fingerprint of the mutable normalized content or requirement blocks. A Store edit could therefore alter the semantics while retaining the declared hash.
- Added `semanticInputHash` to each new `SourceSnapshot`, computed from source type/title/content, normalized blocks, attachment identity/content hashes, and source warnings. Trial validation recomputes the fingerprint and checks the current source revision/hash/requirement-set pointer. A workspace-key HMAC now authenticates the complete snapshot metadata, preventing a Store edit from replacing both source content and its fingerprint; legacy snapshots without the fingerprint or tag fail closed when the production authenticator is active while remaining readable.

## 2026-10-22 Runtime Oracle Validation Regression

- Auditing conformance evaluation found a runtime-integrity gap: TypeScript limited `BusinessOracle.operator`, but `evaluateConformance` compared actual and expected values for any persisted operator. A malformed stored operator could therefore use equality behavior and produce a conformance result.
- Added fail-closed runtime checks for supported operators, non-empty expected values, required previous state for transitions/changes, the visibility value set, and valid requirement/applicability/expectation/observation/execution reference lists. Malformed Reporter assertion/evidence structures and malformed artifact file entries also return `inconclusive` instead of throwing. Oracle evaluation now requires `provenance=real-system` before conformance; unknown or missing provenance is inconclusive, and ScenarioTrust assurance is `none` unless the source is explicitly real-system. Regression cases cover malformed Oracle structures, unknown/unmarked provenance, Reporter arrays, and artifact entries; the approved Oracle remains source-backed and contract-bound.

## 2026-10-26 Acceptance Execution: Explicit Process Paths

- Failure-first review reproduced an incorrect compile when a requirement explicitly started in Draft and targeted Approved, but a single source-ref-matched edge pointed from Draft to Cancelled. The prior compiler could select that edge and derive an Approved assertion without a valid path. After adding StateMachine endpoint resolution, review found the same class of issue in WorkflowModel paths and a further cross-family ambiguity: when both confirmed state and workflow models provided a path for the same explicit endpoints, compilation selected the state route without requiring a model choice.
- Commit `889d5293c8705bee5565a3ffd0c13ff46a7452c8` now resolves explicit state/workflow paths, preserves actors, conditions, business object context, side effects, and source refs, and blocks unreachable, multiple, mixed, or cross-model-family paths instead of selecting a candidate. ## 2026-10-29 Node 22 Reporter failure and local fix

- Exact PR head `256ae631197986c7a1bc6a50af94bc0b594d7a0a` had CI #556 with Node 20 passing and Node 22 failing `src/mcp/realFlow.test.ts`: the strict Playwright JSON reporter was not present on stdout, so the chain correctly failed closed with a reporter Gap. The failing Node 22 job completed **140/141 files**, **1,216 passed / 1 failed / 2 skipped**. This is retained as a candidate failure, not reclassified as a product pass.
- The production Playwright runner now directs JSON Reporter output to a dedicated file via `PLAYWRIGHT_JSON_OUTPUT_FILE`, deletes any prior file before each attempt, parses the file in memory, removes the raw JSON after parsing, and persists only the redacted normalized Reporter artifact. ## 2026-10-29 Raw Playwright Reporter privacy follow-up

- A follow-up review found that the raw Playwright JSON can contain runtime values that should not be exposed as a persisted artifact.
